{{Elections in Illinois}}
The '''2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Illinois''' will be held on November 3, 2026, to elect the 17 [[U.S. representative]]s from the [[U.S. state|state]] of [[Illinois]], one from each of the state's [[Illinois's congressional districts|congressional districts]]. The elections will coincide with [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections|other elections]] to the House of Representatives, [[2026 United States Senate elections|elections]] to the [[United States Senate]], and various [[2026 United States elections|state and local elections]]. Primary elections were held on March 17, 2026.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Illinois State Board of Elections |title=Schedule of Future Elections |url=https://www.elections.il.gov/agencyforms/1%20SCHEDULE%20OF%20FUTURE%20ELECTIONS/Schedule%20of%20Elections%20-%20Revised.pdf |access-date=2025-07-21 |website=www.elections.il.gov}}</ref>

{{see also|Illinois's 1st congressional district}}
The 1st district is based in the [[South Side, Chicago|South Side]] of [[Chicago]], including portions of [[Bronzeville, Chicago|Bronzeville]], [[Hyde Park, Chicago|Hyde Park]], [[Greater Grand Crossing, Chicago|Grand Crossing]], [[Morgan Park, Chicago|Morgan Park]], and [[Roseland, Chicago|Roseland]], but also reaches down to the southwest and takes in a collection of exurban and rural areas in [[Cook County]], [[Will County]], and [[Kankakee County]], including [[New Lenox]] and [[Homer Glen]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Jonathan Jackson (Illinois politician)|Jonathan Jackson]], who was elected with 65.8% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT">{{cite web|url=https://www.cookpolitical.com/vote-tracker/2024/house|title=2024 House Vote Tracker|access-date=December 2, 2024 |website=[[The Cook Political Report with Amy Walter|Cook Political Report]]}}</ref>

==District 2==

{{see also|Illinois's 2nd congressional district}}
The 2nd district includes the far southeast portion of [[Chicago]] and part of its [[Chicago Southland|southern suburbs]], as well as portions of [[Will County, Illinois|Will]], [[Kankakee County, Illinois|Kankakee]], [[Iroquois County, Illinois|Iroquois]], and [[Vermillion County, Illinois|Vermillion]] counties along the [[Indiana]] border. The incumbent is Democrat [[Robin Kelly]], who was re-elected with 67.6% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" /> Kelly retired to unsuccessfully run for the concurrently-held [[2026 United States Senate election in Illinois|U.S. senate election]].<ref name="Robin Senate">{{cite news|last=Petrella|first=Dan|work=[[Chicago Tribune]]|title=US Rep. Robin Kelly jumps into US Senate race to replace retiring Dick Durbin|date=May 6, 2025|url=https://www.chicagotribune.com/2025/05/06/robin-kelly-running-for-senate/}}</ref>

==District 3==
{{Infobox election

{{see also|Illinois's 3rd congressional district}}
The 3rd district is based in northwestern [[Chicago]], including the neighborhoods of [[Albany Park, Chicago|Albany Park]], [[Belmont Cragin, Chicago|Belmont Cragin]], [[Dunning, Chicago|Dunning]], [[Humboldt Park, Chicago|Humboldt Park]], [[Irving Park, Chicago|Irving Park]], [[Logan Square, Chicago|Logan Square]], [[Montclare, Chicago|Montclare]], [[Portage Park, Chicago|Portage Park]], and [[West Town, Chicago|West Town]]. It also includes parts of the [[DuPage County]] suburbs, taking in all or parts of [[Addison, Illinois|Addison]], [[Bartlett, Illinois|Bartlett]], [[Bensenville, Illinois|Bensenville]], [[Elgin, Illinois|Elgin]], [[Elk Grove Village, Illinois|Elk Grove Village]], [[Elmwood Park, Illinois|Elmwood Park]], [[Franklin Park, Illinois|Franklin Park]], [[Glendale Heights, Illinois|Glendale Heights]], [[Hanover Park, Illinois|Hanover Park]], [[River Grove, Illinois|River Grove]], [[Wayne, Illinois|Wayne]], [[West Chicago, Illinois|West Chicago]], [[Wheaton, Illinois|Wheaton]], and [[Wood Dale, Illinois|Wood Dale]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Delia Ramirez]], who was elected with 67.3% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

==District 4==
{{Infobox election
| election_name = 2026 Illinois's 4th congressional district election
| country = Illinois
| type = presidential
| ongoing = yes
| previous_election = 2024 United States House of Representatives elections in Illinois#District 4

| image1 = Patty Garcia with Alderman Andre Vasquez at Fundraiser Cropped.jpg

| party1 = Democratic Party (United States)

| before_election = [[Chuy García]]
| before_party = Democratic Party (United States)
| after_election =

}}
{{see also|Illinois's 4th congressional district}}
The 4th district is based in southwest [[Chicago]], including [[Ashburn, Chicago|Ashburn]], [[Brighton Park, Chicago|Brighton Park]], [[Clearing, Chicago|Clearing]], [[Gage Park, Chicago|Gage Park]], [[Garfield Ridge]], [[South Lawndale]], [[West Elsdon]], and [[West Lawn, Chicago|West Lawn]], and in the western Chicago suburbs, including [[Cicero, Illinois|Cicero]] and [[Berwyn, Illinois|Berwyn]]. The incumbent is retiring-Democrat [[Chuy García]], who was last elected with 67.5% of the vote.<ref name="HPVT" /><ref>{{Cite web |last=Rego |first=Max |title=Democratic Rep. Jesús 'Chuy' García won't seek reelection |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/5587399-chuy-garcia-not-seeking-reelection/ |access-date=November 4, 2025}}</ref>

===Democratic primary===
Incumbent Chuy García, initially filed to run for another term but withdrew his nomination after the deadline to file a nomination passed. This left only one candidate on the Democratic primary ballot; his Chief of Staff, Patty Garcia (no relation). García said he was retiring for health reasons after consulting his doctor, and his wife asking him to retire. Democratic strategist [[David Axelrod]], slammed García's move as Chicago-style "machine tactics" and "election denial of another kind."<ref>{{cite news |last1=Wong |first1=Scott |last2=Stewart |first2=Kyle | title= House rebukes Democratic Rep. Chuy Garcia for succession scheme |url= https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/congress/house-rebukes-democratic-rep-chuy-garcia-succession-scheme-rcna244647 |access-date=November 23, 2025 |work=[[NBC News]] |date=November 18, 2025 |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last1=Wong |first1=Scott | last2=Zanona |first2=Melanie | title= A mad House: Members of Congress trade censures and curse words |url= https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/congress/mad-house-members-congress-trade-censures-curse-words-rcna244829 |access-date=November 23, 2025 |work=[[NBC News]] |date=November 19, 2025 |language=en}}</ref> Democratic representative [[Marie Gluesenkamp Perez]] of Washington introduced a motion of disapproval against García for the alleged scheme.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://washingtonstatestandard.com/2025/11/17/was-marie-gluesenkamp-perez-clashes-with-fellow-democrats-on-house-floor/|title=WA's Marie Gluesenkamp Perez clashes with fellow Democrats on House floor|date=November 17, 2025|accessdate=April 17, 2026|work=[[Washington State Standard]]|first=Jerry|last=Cornfield}}</ref> House Democratic Leader [[Hakeem Jeffries]] urged members to oppose the rebuke, calling García "a progressive champion" and "a good man".<ref>{{Cite web|title=DEMOCRATIC LEADERSHIP STATEMENT ON RESOLUTION OF DISAPPROVAL {{!}} Democratic Leader Hakeem Jeffries|url=http://democraticleader.house.gov/media/press-releases/democratic-leadership-statement-resolution-disapproval|website=democraticleader.house.gov|date=2025-11-18|access-date=2025-12-02|language=en}}</ref> The motion passed 238-186 with mostly Republican votes.<ref>{{cite web|url=https://news.wttw.com/2025/11/18/us-house-set-reprimand-chuy-garcia-over-succession-plan-angering-democrats|title=US House Reprimands Chuy Garcia Over Succession Plan, Angering Democrats|work=[[WTTW]]|date=November 18, 2025|accessdate=April 17, 2025|author=[[Associated Press]]}}</ref>

Several Democrats ran as independents in response to García's actions, though only Chris Getty made the ballot.

====Nominee====
* Patty Garcia, chief of staff of incumbent [[Chuy García]]<ref name="Garcia" />

====Withdrawn====
* [[Chuy García]], incumbent U.S. representative<ref name="Garcia">{{cite news |last1=Sfondeles |first1=Tina |title=U.S. Rep Jesus 'Chuy' Garcia won't seek reelection, leaves seat for chief of staff |url=https://chicago.suntimes.com/elections/2025/11/03/jesus-chuy-garcia-reelection-dropping-out-chief-of-staff-running |access-date=November 3, 2025 |work=[[Chicago Sun-Times]] |date=November 3, 2025 |language=en}}</ref>

==District 5==
{{Infobox election

{{see also|Illinois's 5th congressional district}}
The 5th district includes portions of northern [[Chicago]], including [[Albany Park, Chicago|Albany Park]], [[Forest Glen, Chicago|Forest Glen]], [[Lake View, Chicago|Lake View]], [[Lincoln Park]], [[Lincoln Square, Chicago|Lincoln Square]], [[Norwood Park, Chicago|Norwood Park]], [[North Center]], [[North Park, Chicago|North Park]], [[O'Hare, Chicago|O'Hare]], and [[West Ridge, Chicago|West Ridge]]. It also takes in the northwest Chicago suburbs, including [[Arlington Heights, Illinois|Arlington Heights]] and [[Palatine, Illinois|Palatine]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Mike Quigley]], who was re-elected with 69.0% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

===Democratic primary===
==== Nominee ====
* [[Mike Quigley]], incumbent U.S. representative<ref>{{Cite web |title=FEC Form 2 for Report FEC-1859724 |url=https://docquery.fec.gov/cgi-bin/forms/H0IL05096/1859724/ |access-date=2025-09-20 |website=docquery.fec.gov}}</ref>

==== Eliminated in primary ====
* Matt Conroy, learning and development specialist<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news |last1=McDonald |first1=Maia |title=Chicago gears up as Trump troop deployment looms: 'We aren't helpless' |url=https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2025/sep/05/trump-ice-immigration-chicago-national-guard |access-date=September 6, 2025 |work=[[The Guardian]] |date=September 5, 2025 |quote=Matt Conroy, a Democratic candidate for Illinois's fifth congressional district, recognizes Chicago does have work to do to address crime...}}</ref>

{{see also|Illinois's 6th congressional district}}
The 6th district is based in the southwest [[Chicago]] suburbs, including [[Oak Lawn, Illinois|Oak Lawn]] and [[Downers Grove]], as well as parts of the eastern [[DuPage County]] suburbs. The incumbent is Democrat [[Sean Casten]], who was re-elected with 54.2% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" /> The 2026 election will be a rematch of the 2024 election.

{{see also|Illinois's 7th congressional district}}
The 7th district is based in the [[West Side, Chicago|West Side]] and downtown of [[Chicago]], including [[Austin, Chicago|Austin]], [[East Garfield Park]], the [[Loop, Chicago|Loop]], the [[Near North Side, Chicago|Near North Side]], the [[Near South Side, Chicago|Near South Side]], the [[Near West Side, Chicago|Near West Side]], [[North Lawndale]], [[West Garfield Park]], and [[West Town, Chicago|West Town]]. It also takes in the villages of [[Oak Park, Illinois|Oak Park]] and [[Maywood, Illinois|Maywood]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Danny Davis (Illinois politician)|Danny Davis]], who was re-elected with 83.3% of the vote in 2024,<ref name="HPVT" /> however did not run for re-election in 2026.<ref name="DannyDavis">{{cite news |last=Kapos |first=Shia |date=2025-07-30 |title=Danny Davis to announce he won't seek House reelection |url=https://www.politico.com/live-updates/2025/07/30/congress/danny-davis-retiring-00483259 |access-date=2025-07-30 |website=[[Politico]]}}</ref>

==District 8==
{{Infobox election

{{see also|Illinois's 8th congressional district}}
The 8th district is based in the western outer suburbs of [[Chicago]], including [[Elgin, Illinois|Elgin]], [[Schaumburg, Illinois|Schaumburg]], and [[Des Plaines]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Raja Krishnamoorthi]], who was re-elected with 57.1% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" /> Krishnamoorthi did not seek re-election, instead running unsuccessfully for the [[2026 United States Senate election in Illinois|U.S. Senate in 2026]].<ref name="AlreadyBegun">{{cite news |last1=Huiskes |first1=Helen |title=The Race for Dick Durbin's Senate Seat Has Already Begun |url=https://www.notus.org/senate/illinois-dick-durbin-senate |access-date=January 27, 2025 |work=NOTUS |date=January 27, 2025}}</ref>

===Democratic primary===
====Nominee====
* [[Melissa Bean]], former U.S. representative (2005–2011)<ref>{{Cite web |last=Peterson |first=Eric |date=September 11, 2025 |title=Melissa Bean enters 8th Congressional race, 15 years after loss to Joe Walsh |url=https://www.dailyherald.com/20250911/news/melissa-bean-enters-8th-congressional-race-15-years-after-loss-to-joe-walsh/ |access-date=2025-09-13 |website=[[Daily Herald (Arlington Heights, Illinois)|Daily Herald]]}}</ref>

{{See also|Illinois's 9th congressional district}}
The 9th district is based in northern [[Chicago]] and the [[North Shore (Chicago)|North Shore]], taking in [[Evanston, Illinois|Evanston]] and [[Skokie, Illinois|Skokie]]. The longtime incumbent is Democrat [[Jan Schakowsky]], who was initially elected to the seat in 1998, and re-elected with 68.4% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" /> Schakowsky is not running for re-election.<ref name=15th/>

===Democratic primary===
====Nominee====
* [[Daniel Biss]], [[List of mayors of Evanston, Illinois|mayor]] of [[Evanston, Illinois|Evanston]] (2021–present), [[Illinois Senate|state senator]] from the 9th district (2013–2019), and candidate for [[Governor of Illinois|governor]] in [[2018 Illinois gubernatorial election|2018]]<ref>{{cite news |last1=Eadie |first1=Matthew |title=Mayor Biss to run for Congress |url=https://evanstonnow.com/mayor-biss-to-run-for-congress/ |access-date=May 15, 2025 |work=Evanston Now |date=May 15, 2025}}</ref>

==District 10==

{{see also|Illinois's 10th congressional district}}
The 10th district is based in the northern suburbs and exurbs of [[Chicago]], including [[Mundelein, Illinois|Mundelein]], [[Northbrook, Illinois|Northbrook]], and [[Waukegan]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Brad Schneider]], who was re-elected with 60.0% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

===Democratic primary===
====Nominee====
* [[Brad Schneider]], incumbent U.S. representative<ref name=":2" />

====Eliminated in primary====
* Morgan Coghill, plywood importer<ref name="Progressive Mayor"/>

{{see also|Illinois's 11th congressional district}}
The 11th district is based in the western suburbs and exurbs of [[Chicago]], including [[Aurora, Illinois|Aurora]], [[Naperville, Illinois|Naperville]], and [[Belvidere, Illinois|Belvidere]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Bill Foster (politician)|Bill Foster]], who was re-elected with 55.6% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

{{see also|Illinois's 12th congressional district}}
The 12th district is based in [[southern Illinois]], taking in the southeastern [[St. Louis]] exurbs. It includes [[Carbondale, Illinois|Carbondale]], [[Centralia, Illinois|Centralia]], [[Marion, Illinois|Marion]], and [[O'Fallon, Illinois|O'Fallon]]. The incumbent is Republican [[Mike Bost]], who was re-elected with 74.2% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

{{see also|Illinois's 13th congressional district}}
The 13th district is based in [[central Illinois]], stretching from the [[Champaign–Urbana metropolitan area]] to the eastern [[St. Louis]] suburbs and taking in [[Decatur, Illinois|Decatur]] as well as the state capital, [[Springfield, Illinois|Springfield]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Nikki Budzinski]], who was re-elected with 58.1% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

===Democratic primary===
====Nominee====
* [[Nikki Budzinski]], incumbent U.S. representative<ref name=":2" />

The 14th district is based in the western exurbs of [[Chicago]], including all or parts of [[Aurora, Illinois|Aurora]], [[DeKalb, Illinois|DeKalb]], [[Granville, Illinois|Granville]], [[Joliet, Illinois|Joliet]], [[Montgomery, Illinois|Montgomery]], [[Naperville, Illinois|Naperville]], [[Oswego, Illinois|Oswego]], [[Ottawa, Illinois|Ottawa]], [[Peru, Illinois|Peru]], [[Plainfield, Illinois|Plainfield]], [[Shorewood, Illinois|Shorewood]], [[Spring Valley, Illinois|Spring Valley]], [[Sugar Grove, Illinois|Sugar Grove]], and [[Sycamore, Illinois|Sycamore]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Lauren Underwood]], who was re-elected with 55.1% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

===Republican primary===
====Nominee====

The 16th district is based in the rural areas of [[northern Illinois]]. The incumbent is Republican [[Darin LaHood]], who was re-elected unopposed in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

==District 17==
{{Infobox election

{{see also|Illinois's 17th congressional district}}
The 17th district is based in north-central Illinois, stretching from [[Rockford, Illinois|Rockford]] to the [[Quad Cities metropolitan area]] to [[Bloomington, Illinois|Bloomington]], also taking in [[Peoria, Illinois|Peoria]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Eric Sorensen (politician)|Eric Sorensen]], who was elected with 54.4% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT" />

====Nominee====
